# Agent harness-fit verification — axis-4 of convergence-gate (POINTER-INDEX)

# Reference: TIER-1 Z:/repos/deps/superpowers/skills/verification-before-completion/SKILL.md:1-20 @ HEAD e7a2d16476bf042e9add4699c9d018a90f86e4a6 [VERIFIED 2026-05-04 per mia-pre-apply.md TIER-1 anchor — already-settled sister cite reused] — obra/superpowers Iron Law "verify EVIDENCE before claims"; the SOTA root pattern this rule operationalizes at convergence-gate Axis-4 (harness-fit verification) for agent-emitted ship-candidates BEFORE T1 consult OR ship queue
# Reference: TIER-2 .claude/projects/Z--claude-sota-installed/memory/feedback_sota_researcher_harness_fit_verification_2026_05_02.md (n=5 promotion-source memory; this rule is the codification per `.claude/rules/codification-threshold.md` cycle-322 jurisdiction n>=3 self-observed promotion bar)
# Reference: TIER-2 .claude/rules/convergence-gate.md (Axis 1+2+3 — this rule adds Axis 4 harness-fit as the scoring layer agents currently miss)
# Reference: TIER-2 .claude/rules/synthesis-layer-verify.md §Subclaim-type discriminator (UPSTREAM-CLAIM / OPERATIONAL-CLAIM / CATEGORY-CLAIM — agents must classify candidate type before harness-fit probe)
# Reference: TIER-2 .claude/rules/codification-threshold.md cycle-322 jurisdiction (n>=3 self-observed promotes feedback→rule; n=5 satisfied)

# TIER-3 evidence-trail provenance only: `.claude/projects/Z--claude-sota-installed/memory/feedback_sota_researcher_harness_fit_verification_2026_05_02.md` carries the 5-instance evidence ladder + per-class analysis; this rule is the operational mechanism that file's evidence promotes.

## The rule

Sota-researcher / architect / general agent dispatches that surface SOTA candidates MUST include a **harness-fit verification step** asking "is this applicable to OUR specific architecture?" — beyond the convergence-gate Axis 1+2+3 (T1 sources / T2 practitioners / Axis 3 stability) that scores SOTA-status. Without harness-fit, candidates pass convergence-gate AND still fail at ship-target verification because of architectural mismatch invisible to upstream-source-only audit.

This is **convergence-gate Axis 4** (harness-fit) — separate from Axis 1+2+3 (which classify SOTA-status of the candidate ITSELF) and applied AFTER convergence-gate PASS, BEFORE ship-target verification.

## When this rule applies

Whenever an agent (sota-researcher / architect / general-purpose / Explore / code-reviewer / debugger) returns a ship-candidate ranking from a Wave-N dispatch:

1. The agent's convergence-gate verdict is necessary BUT NOT SUFFICIENT for ship-readiness
2. The candidate may be valid SOTA AND inapplicable to claude-sota
3. Apply the 4-axis harness-fit probe BEFORE writing T1 consult prompt OR queuing the ship

**F-003 ordering note**: in source file, §Codex-rescue verdict provenance preceded §Codex-rescue blind-spot specialization, and provenance text referenced "per FM-09 specialization above". Per Wave 159 SB5 codex T1 F-003 prescription, the order is REVERSED in `ahfv-codex-rescue-blind-spot.md` (blind-spot FIRST, then verdict provenance) so the forward-reference resolves correctly. Section labels themselves are PRESERVED verbatim per F-004.

## Anti-patterns

- **Trusting agent ranking verbatim without 4-axis probe** — refuted by n=5 same-arc REJECTs. The agent's PASS verdict is necessary but NOT sufficient.
- **Skipping probe 4 because "the upstream repo is local"** — local clone existence ≠ ship-readiness. ECC plugin EXPOSES many ECC artifacts as plugin-loaded skills; vendoring them again duplicates.
- **Promoting at n=1 without classifying the failure into one of the 7 sub-classes** — refuted by `codification-threshold.md` cycle-322 jurisdiction; class identification is what makes promotion durable.
- **Applying Axis 4 BEFORE Axis 1+2+3** — the order matters: SOTA classification is necessary upstream context; harness-fit is the downstream filter. A non-SOTA candidate doesn't need Axis 4 (already REJECTED).
- **Adopting any installer marked HARD-GATE / `disable-model-invocation: true` / interactive setup gate** — refuted by iter-84 brainstorming + iter-92 mattpocock cohort (n=2 same-pattern). HARD-GATE installers structurally break autonomous /loop mode at install time; setup interaction is NOT compatible with `defaultMode: "auto"` permission classifier. CR-7 Phase 2/3 transition predicates do NOT relax this — the install-time HARD-GATE fires BEFORE permission mode applies. **Reject the current install shape, NOT the project forever** — if upstream removes interactive gates and ships canonical `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json`, fresh Probe 4/5/6 + SRA D6/T1 re-verification can promote install-class disposition.

# Agent harness-fit Probe DAG (4-axis probe + Probes 1-7 + Phase 7 benchmark gate)

# === Cite authority inherited from parent header (F-002 compact Authority Excerpt) ===
# Reference: TIER-2 .claude/projects/Z--claude-sota-installed/memory/feedback_sota_researcher_harness_fit_verification_2026_05_02.md (n=5 promotion-source memory)
# Reference: TIER-2 .claude/rules/convergence-gate.md (Axis 1+2+3 — this rule adds Axis 4 harness-fit)
# Reference: TIER-2 .claude/rules/synthesis-layer-verify.md §Subclaim-type discriminator
# Reference: TIER-2 .claude/rules/codification-threshold.md cycle-322 jurisdiction
# Reference: TIER-2 .claude/rules/kiss-dry-yagni.md Must-Never #4
# TIER-3 evidence-trail provenance only: source memory carries the per-class analysis

## Authority Excerpt (from parent quick-reference)

This child owns the **Probe DAG** — the 7 individual probe procedures (Probe 1 count-OVER / Probe 2 SDK-vs-CLI / Probe 3 architectural-API / Probe 4 plugin-namespace / Probe 5 mode-harness-shape / Probe 6 direct-file/registry blockers / Probe 7 demand-gate split with .a/.b sub-classes) PLUS the §Phase 7 benchmark gate for ADOPT-NOW multi-agent kits (Wave 138 Fire 2 codification). Sister-rule routing: 7 sub-classes catalog (`ahfv-seven-sub-classes.md`) → Probe DAG (this child) → FM-09 Codex-rescue blind-spot specialization (`ahfv-codex-rescue-blind-spot.md`).

Parent index: `agent-harness-fit-verification.md §Pointer index`

## How to apply (4-axis probe)

Before declaring an agent's ship-candidate "ready":

### Probe 1 — count-OVER

If the candidate claims a count delta (commit count, file count, line count, star count): verify against authoritative local source (`git log`, `wc -l`, `Glob | wc -l`, `mcp__github__search_repositories` star refresh) BEFORE accepting. Agent counts via remote APIs can drift, paginate-truncate, or include stale cache.

### Probe 2 — SDK-vs-CLI surface

If the candidate proposes adopting an SDK feature / config flag / env var: verify the **invocation surface** exists in claude-sota:
- Does claude-sota use the SDK or CLI? Check `.claude/hooks/scripts/*.py` + `.claude/settings.json` env block
- Does the candidate's mechanism map to claude-sota's actual mode (interactive vs print-mode vs SDK-managed-agent)?
- If the candidate requires a mode claude-sota doesn't run in, REJECT-for-fit even if SOTA-valid upstream

### Probe 3 — architectural-API

If the candidate proposes a primitive (cache_control, prompt-cache, structured-outputs, batch-API): verify the **API ecosystem match**:
- Anthropic-API primitive vs OpenAI-API primitive vs vendor-neutral OTel
- claude-sota's gpt5.5/codex hooks talk to OpenAI-API endpoints; Anthropic-only primitives don't apply there
- Cross-check before assuming "this Anthropic pattern works on codex"

### Probe 5 — mode-harness-shape

If the candidate proposes adopting a skill/workflow/pattern with operational mode dependencies (HARD-GATE on user interaction, interactive-vs-autonomous mismatch, meta-skill assumptions about sister-harness presence, file-count or size-sprawl orthogonal to existing layout): verify the **operational mode + harness shape match**:
- Does the candidate skill HARD-GATE on interactive user approval? Verify against claude-sota's autonomous /loop mode (incompatible)
- Does the candidate require a sister-skill or sister-harness present in upstream's framework but absent locally? (e.g., Superpowers TDD-for-skills test framework)
- Does verbatim port introduce file count or size disproportionate to existing layer? (e.g., a single skill bringing 7+ files when sibling skills are 1-3 files)
- If ANY mode/harness assumption is unmet, REJECT-FOR-FIT even if SOTA-valid upstream + Axis 1+2+3 PASS

### Probe 4 — plugin-namespace

If the candidate proposes vendoring an artifact from upstream (ECC, codex-plugin-cc, anthropics-cookbook): check **plugin-loaded skill namespace** FIRST:
- `everything-claude-code:<artifact>` exposed via plugin? → DUPLICATE if vendored, REJECT per kiss-dry-yagni Must-Never #4
- `codex:<artifact>` exposed via codex plugin? → DUPLICATE
- `<plugin>:<artifact>` only available as legacy-shim? → still plugin-exposed
- Only proceed with vendoring if the artifact is NOT in any loaded plugin namespace

**Where to check**: the available-skills list injected into agent context via system-reminder (lists every `<plugin>:<skill>` entry). Agents can also `mcp__github__get_file_contents` on the plugin's `marketplace.json` to enumerate exposed commands.

### Probe 6 — supplementary direct-file/registry blockers (FM-09 promotion iter-67)

If the candidate proposes adopting an upstream artifact (skill, MCP server, plugin, library, vendored pattern) whose viability depends on:
- License compatibility (claude-sota is permissive-license-only — MIT / Apache-2.0 / BSD acceptable; AGPLv3 / GPLv3 / SSPL / proprietary REJECT)
- README badge / status banner (archive-status, deprecation notice, "moved to maintenance mode" warning)
- Published-package existence (npm / PyPI / cargo / go-modules registry)
- Build-deps availability (Cargo / GCC / Clang / Python venv requirement)

VERIFY via direct-file/registry probes:
- **LICENSE file read** at upstream root: `cat Z:/repos/deps/<repo>/LICENSE | head -5` — match against permissive-license whitelist
- **README badge inspection**: `rg -n "img.shields.io/badge/license|maintenance|archived|deprecated" Z:/repos/deps/<repo>/README.md`
- **Registry direct-existence probe**: `curl -sS https://registry.npmjs.org/<pkg-encoded>` (`{"error":"Not found"}` = phantom) OR `curl https://pypi.org/pypi/<pkg>/json` (404 = phantom)
- **Build-deps probe**: `which cargo gcc clang` OR check `pyproject.toml` / `package.json` for unsupported dep stack

If ANY direct-file/registry probe surfaces a blocker, REJECT-FOR-FIT even if SOTA-valid upstream + Axis 1+2+3 PASS + Probe 1-5 PASS. The blocker is STRUCTURAL — no operational mitigation exists.

**Empirical evidence n=2 (FM-09 ladder)**: Memory/RAG audit (openviking AGPLv3 LICENSE blocker — `reference_memory_rag_audit_HNF_agplv3_blocker_2026_05_02.md:52-64`); Ship D (ast-grep MCP phantom @anthropic/mcp-ast-grep npm package — `reference_ship_d_ast_grep_phantom_npm_HNF_2026_05_02.md`).

### Probe 7 — demand-gate split (NEW 2026-05-03; .a/.b sub-class added 2026-05-05 Wave 27 per Agent E §Issue 1 catch-22)

If the candidate proposes adopting a primitive (MCP / skill / library / tool) that is structurally sound (Probes 1-6 PASS): verify the **harness-side operational driver** BEFORE adoption. Probe 7 splits into two outcomes — `.a` REJECT (demand-absence) and `.b` STUDY-PILOT eligible (demand-creates-new-workflow). The split closes the Wave 27 catch-22 surfaced at `tmp/wave27-agentE-saturation-adversarial-2026-05-05.md:46-56,89-106` (Probe 7.a as currently worded auto-REJECTs any primitive that creates a NEW workflow because no sss invocation surface yet exists — circular per Jeffries YAGNI canonical statement at `kiss-dry-yagni.md:31-33`).

**Hard precondition (before applying .a or .b)**: earlier Axis-4 blockers WIN. If the candidate duplicates an existing shipped primitive (Probe 4 plugin-namespace), collides with plugin namespace, requires an unmet harness/mode assumption (Probe 5 mode-harness-shape), fails Probe 6 (license/registry/build-deps blocker), is SUPERSEDED-BY-X (existing primitive owns the workflow), or is a META-HARNESS competing-framework shape per `docs/verified-avoid.md` Cohort 1, classify under that blocker instead. Probe 7.b is ONLY available for structurally sound primitives that create a genuinely new, non-duplicative workflow.

#### Probe 7.a — DEMAND-ABSENCE (REJECT-FOR-FIT)

What sss workflow / invocation surface / consumer would route through this primitive TODAY? Verify the 3-clause check:
- Cite a specific path (existing or queued)
- Is the candidate's value proposition met by an EXISTING shipped primitive? (e.g., "graphiti MCP already provides FalkorDB temporal KG access" → dbhub adds no marginal value AS A REPLACEMENT)
- Is the candidate displacing or extending? Displacement requires migration plan; extension requires marginal-value evidence

If NO current/queued workflow AND .b 5-clause check (below) does not survive, REJECT-FOR-FIT.a (demand-absence) — REJECT-decisive.

#### Probe 7.b — DEMAND-CREATES-NEW-WORKFLOW (STUDY-PILOT eligible)

A structurally sound primitive may create a genuinely new workflow that no existing sss primitive serves. STUDY-PILOT eligibility requires ALL 5 clauses to hold (codex T1 NEEDS-REVISION conf=0.84 Q2 prescription at `.claude/state/codex_consult_ship27_3_probe7_demand_creates_OUT.txt`):

1. **Named operational use case**: cite the query/workflow and the consumer that would exercise it
2. **Cited local input/source path**: identify the concrete data, repo path, tool surface, or state file (e.g., `Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/state/mcp_overhead_audit.jsonl`)
3. **Wiring path**: name the adapter/ETL/config needed to make the primitive consume the source; if wiring is non-trivial, it is part of the pilot cost
4. **Incumbent comparison**: explain why existing shipped primitives or simpler local commands (`rg`, `jq`, GitNexus, Langfuse MCP, graphiti, mcp-memory, one-off Python scripts) do not already satisfy the use case OR are materially worse
5. **Reversible time-box**: define owner, max pilot cost, 30-day success criterion, and retirement path

**Pilot failure**: retire to `disabledMcpjsonServers` if applicable and document in `docs/verified-avoid.md` under the matching failure cohort; create a dedicated retired-pilot / demand-not-realized cohort only after n>=2 same-shape recurrences. NOTE: `verified-avoid.md` Cohort 2 is specifically Probe 6 direct-file/registry blockers per `docs/verified-avoid.md:47-49`; failed demand-pilots do NOT belong there by default. Use the matching failure cohort for retirement (e.g., a pilot that failed because it duplicated an existing primitive retires to Cohort 1 META-HARNESS competing-frameworks).

**Example (dbhub Wave 27 caveat — ETL-explicit)**: bytebase/dbhub could enable SQL analytics over a generated SQLite audit warehouse derived from `.claude/state/*.jsonl`; the pilot must include the JSONL → SQLite ingestion path plus at least one committed query/report consumer. Without that ETL path, dbhub is NOT sufficient evidence for Probe 7.b — DBHub's official surface (`https://dbhub.ai/` + `bytebase/dbhub@72adfdcf` README + `server.json`) supports configured Postgres/MySQL/SQL Server/MariaDB/SQLite databases via SQL tools, NOT direct JSONL querying. Wave 27 codex T1 verified-via codex secondary probe of DBHub introduction page + TOML config docs + GitHub MCP-fetched README/server.json [VERIFIED 2026-05-05 via `.claude/state/codex_consult_ship27_3_probe7_demand_creates_OUT.txt`].

**Harness-fit discriminator for REJECT vs STUDY-PILOT outcomes** (renamed from "4-class harness-fit-FAIL discriminator" per Wave 27 codex T1 Q1 prescription — `.b` is non-fail; original Q3 prescription at `.claude/state/codex_consult_axis4_subclass_taxonomy_probe7_demand_absence_OUT.txt:3825-3829` 2026-05-03; .a/.b split per Wave 27 codex T1 verdict at `.claude/state/codex_consult_ship27_3_probe7_demand_creates_OUT.txt` 2026-05-05): use BEFORE classifying a verdict to ensure the right Probe is invoked.

- `DEMAND-ABSENCE.a` (Probe 7.a): no current, queued, or counterfactual workflow survives the named-use-case check — REJECT-FOR-FIT
- `DEMAND-CREATES-NEW-WORKFLOW.b` (Probe 7.b, NON-FAIL CLASS): structurally sound primitive creates a named, non-duplicative workflow; STUDY-PILOT eligible only after the 5-clause check passes
- `SUPERSEDED-BY-X` (related to Probes 4/5): a workflow exists, but an incumbent primitive already owns it or was selected by prior verdict (e.g., cognee → Graphiti L3 verdict)
- `PLUGIN-NAMESPACE` (Probe 4): the artifact already exists in a loaded plugin namespace
- `MODE-HARNESS-SHAPE` (Probe 5): candidate requires an operating mode or harness assumption sss does not satisfy

**Empirical evidence**: n=1 fresh self-observed (bytebase/dbhub REJECT-FOR-FIT 0.85, 2026-05-03 — Agent task output records "no application-DB query workflow"; existing DB-adjacent MCPs cover all live use cases) + n=1 retro corroboration (apollo-graphql per parent `Z:/claude/.claude/projects/Z--claude/memory/reference_mcp_disabled_rationale.md:20`). 4 historical disabled MCPs (claude-context/grafana/opik/local-judge) NOT counted under this Probe — they are CREDENTIAL-GATED-PENDING / A/B-PENDING / SUPERSEDED-BY-X / sss-config-drift distinct classes per the discriminator above.

**Wave 27 caveat (2026-05-05)**: dbhub may be DEMAND-CREATES-NEW-WORKFLOW.b for audit-log SQL analytics, but ONLY with an explicit JSONL → SQLite/Postgres ETL path. DBHub does not currently provide direct JSONL querying. The Wave 27 .b codification specifically opens a pilot pathway IF the operator commits to building the ETL surface — without it, dbhub still falls under Probe 7.a REJECT-FOR-FIT.

Anti-pattern: adopting a primitive because it's SOTA-valid + plugin-namespace-clean + license-compatible WITHOUT identifying a single sss workflow that consumes it. Result is MCP slot occupation without operational driver — historically the disabled-MCP cohort pattern (n=6 in `manifests/services.yaml:80-98` plus `.claude/settings.json:217` `disabledMcpjsonServers`).

Anti-pattern (Probe 7.b bypass — Wave 27 codification): claiming `.b` STUDY-PILOT eligibility for a candidate that fails any earlier Axis-4 blocker (PLUGIN-NAMESPACE / MODE-HARNESS-SHAPE / SUPERSEDED-BY-X / Probe 6 / META-HARNESS competing-framework shape per `docs/verified-avoid.md` Cohort 1). The `.b` escape hatch is admissible ONLY for structurally sound primitives — see Hard precondition above. Without the precondition gate, `.b` becomes a bypass for any attractive new primitive that duplicates `team-lead` / 12-agent fleet / T1-T7 lifecycle / etc.

### Phase 7 — benchmark gate for ADOPT-NOW multi-agent kits (Wave 138 Fire 2 codification per real GPT-5.5 codex T1 NEEDS-REVISION conf=0.88 prescription verbatim)

If a multi-agent kit or plugin bundle receives `ADOPT-NOW`, require `BENCHMARK-PASS` evidence before install/enable. Acceptable evidence is a reproducible benchmark repo, inline benchmark fixtures with commands, or a local eval artifact tied to the claimed improvement. If the README makes >=3 numeric improvement claims without reproducible methodology, route through `Z:/claude-sota/.claude/rules/convergence-gate.md:127-149` Row-2 fabrication-test FAIL.

**Trigger scope** (Voice 1 Q5 caveat): fires ONLY on ADOPT-NOW install/enable for multi-agent kits or plugin bundles. Does NOT fire on:
- Descriptive doc imports (cite-import-AMBER per CLAUDE.md Section 14.5)
- Single-rule / single-skill cite-anchors
- Read-only research probes (per cardinal-rule-9 read-only research probe exception)

**Empirical evidence anchor**: Wave 138 Fire 1 wshobson Voice 2 sota-researcher REJECT-FOR-FIT-MAJORITY (76/80 = 95% rejection) demonstrated the gap — STUDY-PILOT-NARROW survivors (3 candidates: protect-mcp + signed-audit-trails + shell-scripting) need fabrication-test verification BEFORE install commit per `convergence-gate.md` Row-2.

**Cite anchor**: TIER-2 sister-rule cite-import per CLAUDE.md Section 14.5 — `convergence-gate.md:120-149 @ Z:/claude-sota` + Voice 1 codex T1 verdict at `.claude/state/codex_consult_w138f1_3axis_ship_OUT.txt:3001-3008` (verbatim Phase 7 block) + `.claude/state/codex_consult_w138f2_doconly_bundle_OUT.txt` (Wave 138 Fire 2 codification verdict NEEDS-REVISION conf=0.88 + insertion-line correction L111→L156).

name: convergence-gate
description: Adoption gate — new patterns/repos/SDKs require convergence evidence (≥3 T1 sources + ≥2 named T2 practitioners + ≥3 months stability OR strong-provenance-express predicate), else REJECT-until-convergence

# Convergence Gate — Adopt Only When Pattern Has Converged

# TIER-3 port provenance only: ported from Z:/claude/rules/convergence-gate.md HEAD. Parent CCC retains cycle-N audit evidence; claude-sota authority is the TIER-1/TIER-2 sources cited inside this rule.

## The rule
A new pattern, repo, SDK, or external primitive is ADOPT-NOW only when CONVERGENCE evidence is present. Convergence means the pattern exists independently in multiple authoritative places, not just one. Absent convergence, the default verdict is **REJECT-until-convergence** — not STUDY-PILOT, not "maybe later."

This gate stacks ABOVE the `.claude/skills/sota-research/SKILL.md` 5-row LOAD-BEARING bar (LOCAL — sota-research skill present at `.claude/skills/sota-research/SKILL.md`). It is stricter: the 5-row bar allows STUDY-PILOT on any `[UNKNOWN]`; the convergence gate does not — any missing convergence axis is a REJECT until that axis is satisfied OR the STRONG-PROVENANCE-EXPRESS predicate fires.

## Convergence axes (ALL three required for ADOPT-NOW)

| Axis | Threshold | Evidence shape |
|---|---|---|
| **Independent T1 sources** | ≥3 distinct orgs/repos/papers implementing the pattern | file:line cites in 3 different `Z:/repos/deps/<repo>/` dirs OR 3 different official docs URLs — not 3 pages of the same source |
| **Named T2 practitioners** | ≥2 named practitioners publicly adopting | Recognizable engineers (Karpathy / Simon Willison / Boris Cherny / Anthropic staff / equivalents), with a dated artifact (tweet, blog post, commit, conf talk) citing THIS specific pattern — not generic category endorsement |
| **Stability** | ≥3 months from pattern emergence to evaluation date | first public commit/post ≥90d old; <90d = "new and shiny," re-evaluate after the burn-in period (UNLESS STRONG-PROVENANCE-EXPRESS applies — see §Axis 3 5-band table) |

Any axis failing = REJECT-until-convergence. Verdict line must name the specific missing axis + the exact evidence that would satisfy it.

## Terminology disambiguation

**Convergence-gate Axis 3** = **stability** (age + cpd; quantitative scalar measuring repo/pattern maturity). About: "is this pattern old enough to trust?"

**`.claude/rules/synthesis-layer-verify.md` Subclaim-type discriminator** (UPSTREAM-CLAIM / OPERATIONAL-CLAIM / CATEGORY-CLAIM) = **claim-source classification** (qualitative shape; about what's being verified, not when). About: "which kind of evidence does this claim require?"

Same word "axis" appears in both rules referring to different concepts. Use full names ("Axis 3 stability" vs "subclaim-type discriminator") when ambiguity matters.

## How to apply

Before writing any ADOPT verdict in `reports/sota-*.md` or `reports/*-audit*.md`:

1. **List the 3 T1 sources explicitly**. If you cannot name 3 different `Z:/repos/deps/<repo>/file:line` OR 3 different official docs URLs where the pattern appears independently, the axis fails.
2. **Name the 2 T2 practitioners explicitly with dated artifact**. "Karpathy endorses" without a tweet URL + date is not evidence; it's wishful.
3. **Check the stability clock**. First commit / first public post date — `mcp__github__get_file_contents` on first-commit metadata, or the earliest blog post / paper date. <90d = candidate for STRONG-PROVENANCE-EXPRESS only (see §Axis 3); else fail.
4. **If ANY axis fails**, write `REJECT-until-convergence` as the verdict, with a bullet list specifying exactly what evidence would close each missing axis.
5. **Only then** run the sota-research 5-row bar. The convergence gate is a precondition — passing it doesn't auto-ADOPT, but failing it auto-REJECTS.

## Evaluation methodology — concrete tools per axis

Server-family availability per `.claude/rules/research-protocol.md:52-69` Tool Routing (iter-2 port). Function-level support marked `use-if-available` where the iter-2 routing table didn't independently verify the specific call shape.

### Axis 1 — Independent T1 sources (≥3 distinct)

Parallel enumeration via complementary tools — each catches what the others miss:

| Tool family | Strength | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `mcp__github__search_code` / `mcp__github__get_file_contents` | Exact symbol / import-pattern matches across GitHub | ACTIVE |
| `mcp__octocode__githubSearchCode` | Cross-repo code search with stable query identifiers — substitute: `mcp__github__search_code` | REMOVED (2026-05-03 Round-5d; FM-04 D6 firm) |
| `mcp__deepwiki__read_wiki_structure` + `read_wiki_contents` | AI-generated structured wiki per repo — surfaces patterns not in README keywords | ACTIVE (use-if-available; structure/content calls returned 404 on some repos in prior cycles) |
| `mcp__deepwiki__ask_question` | Repo-level Q&A without reading every file | ACTIVE — verify-only, not sole evidence |
| `mcp__repomix__pack_remote_repository` + `grep_repomix_output` | Cross-file pattern scan inside one repo | ACTIVE |
| `mcp__context7__resolve-library-id` + `query-docs` | Official docs as T1 (when adopting a library) | ACTIVE |
| `mcp__exa__web_search_exa` (category:research paper) | Peer-reviewed papers + named-org blogs as T1 | ACTIVE |
| `mcp__firecrawl__firecrawl_scrape` / `firecrawl_search` | Live-scrape when target is JS-rendered or behind soft paywall | ACTIVE |

**Rule**: at least 3 of these must return positive evidence for the SAME pattern from 3 DIFFERENT orgs/repos/papers. Not 3 different tools on the same source.

### Axis 2 — Named T2 practitioners (≥2 with dated artifact)

| Tool family | Use | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `mcp__exa__web_search_exa` (category:people, full name + pattern) | Find blog posts / talks by named practitioners | ACTIVE |
| `mcp__perplexity__perplexity_search` (recency filter: month/year) | Fresh endorsements — recognizable practitioners mentioning the pattern | ACTIVE |
| `mcp__firecrawl__firecrawl_scrape` on social profiles | Verify a claimed tweet/post actually exists and is dated | ACTIVE |
| `mcp__github__get_me` / search-users / repo collaborator metadata | Employer / org affiliation signals | ACTIVE |
| `mcp__deepwiki__ask_question` on the candidate repo | "Who are the major users or contributors from named orgs?" — triangulate | ACTIVE — use-if-available |

**Mandatory step — WebFetch README + grep practitioners**: GraphQL/`mcp__github__` collaborator metadata is necessary but **not sufficient** for axis 2. Named-practitioner endorsements frequently live in README content (direct quotes, acknowledgments, case studies) rather than repo-collaborator metadata. Before scoring axis 2, ALWAYS:
1. `WebFetch` (or `mcp__firecrawl__firecrawl_scrape`) the README
2. Grep for named-practitioner tokens recognizable in the relevant ecosystem
3. Require URL + date on each citation per the base rule

**Bidirectional**: the README-grep step upgrades verdicts when collaborator-metadata missed a signal AND confirms-REJECT when README also shows zero. Without the README-grep step, canonical repos with direct practitioner endorsements can be scored [UNKNOWN] → REJECT incorrectly.

**Rule**: each claimed practitioner endorsement must have a URL + date. "X tweets about Y" without a link is [UNKNOWN], not T2.

### Axis 3 — Stability (≥3 months first-public-artifact age, scored across 5 bands)

| Tool family | Use | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `mcp__github__get_file_contents` / commit history | `createdAt` / first-commit on the repo — repo age | ACTIVE |
| `mcp__exa__web_search_exa` (date range) | Earliest blog post / paper mentioning the pattern | ACTIVE |
| `mcp__arxiv__search_papers` + `get_abstract` | Paper publication date for research-backed patterns | ACTIVE |

**Rule**: 90 days minimum from EARLIEST public artifact, not from the version you're looking at. A v2.0 release isn't stability evidence if v1.0 was 30 days ago.

**Supplementary signal — commits/day (cpd)**: raw age alone under-specifies stability. A 99-day-old repo shipping 15 commits/day is structurally different from a 99-day-old repo shipping 1 commit/week — the former is still in active iteration, not burn-in. Compute `cpd = total_commits / age_days` alongside age:

| cpd × age band | Classification | Implication |
|---|---|---|
| `cpd < 10` AND `age ≥ 90d` | Stable burn-in | Firm axis-3 PASS |
| `10 ≤ cpd ≤ 20` AND `90d ≤ age ≤ 180d` | Active iteration | Borderline axis-3 — PASS-with-caveat; downgrade to STUDY-PILOT or CONDITIONAL-ADOPT if axis-2 also PARTIAL |
| `cpd > 10` AND `age > 180d` | **Sustained active maintenance** | Firm axis-3 PASS — mature age proves stability AND velocity signals ongoing investment, NOT rewrite churn. Classic shape: high-velocity repos past burn-in (avoid misclassifying as fast-churn anti-pattern) |
| `cpd > 10` AND `age < 100d` | Fast-churn anti-pattern | Treat age-PASS as borderline, expect rewrites, re-audit after +90d |
| **STRONG-PROVENANCE-EXPRESS** (5th band, predicate-gated): `age ≥ 30d` AND axis-1 = official-org maintainership AND axis-2 = named-T2 endorsement OR maintainer-org IS T2-equivalent | **Firm axis-3 PASS — relaxed maturity gate** | When provenance is strong (org-level T1) AND a named-T2 OR org-equivalent endorsement exists, the launch-spike risk that motivates the strict 90-day gate is dominated by maintainer-org commitment. Examples of org-level T1 patterns: vercel-labs / anthropics / openai / getzep / langchain / microsoft / google / facebook-research / google-deepmind / openai-evals / equivalents. The LAUNCH-SPIKE band still applies for `unknown-org + age<90d + cpd>50` — protection holds for the truly anti-pattern cases (solo-org launch with non-permissive license = REJECT-UNTIL-CONVERGENCE unchanged) |

The bands are exhaustive — every candidate falls into exactly one. STRONG-PROVENANCE-EXPRESS is a predicate-gated relaxation, not a default fallback; verify all three predicate clauses (≥30d age, official-org maintainer, named-T2 or org-equivalent endorsement) before applying it.

### Fusion rule (Rider 4 discipline)

No single tool is authoritative. Running any ONE of the above in isolation produces narrow-grep false-negatives (single-tool signal ≠ convergence). The convergence check requires ≥3 distinct tool-types returning compatible evidence — otherwise treat the finding as "one-tool signal," which is not convergence.

Cost discipline: cheap-first. `mcp__github__` calls (cheap, rate-limited) before `mcp__firecrawl__firecrawl_agent` (multi-minute credits). DeepWiki `ask_question` before packing the whole repo with repomix. If 3 cheap signals converge, don't escalate to expensive ones; if they don't converge, ONE expensive confirmatory signal is worth more than three more cheap ones.

## Anti-pattern: retroactive convergence manufacturing

Do not construct convergence evidence after choosing the verdict. The sequence must be: (1) collect convergence evidence → (2) count axes → (3) verdict. Not (1) pick verdict → (2) find supporting cites.

If a candidate looks promising but fails convergence today, the right move is to **queue a re-audit date** (90 days out, or when a named T2 practitioner is spotted adopting it), not lower the gate.

## Anti-pattern: Row-2 fabrication-test FAIL

When running the 5-row bar's Row 2 (Numeric — measurable improvement), a README presenting **≥3 numeric performance claims without methodology citation** (no repro script link, no benchmark repo, no dataset name) is **auto-FAIL** — do not attempt live A/B to verify, just REJECT. This short-circuits the primary-source rule: vendor marketing numbers that aren't backed by a reproducible artifact are false-by-default until proven otherwise.

**Procedure**:
1. Count numeric claims in README that make improvement/speedup assertions
2. For each, check: does the README cite a reproducible artifact (benchmark script path, dataset name, tool+version)?
3. If ≥3 claims lack citations, Row-2 auto-FAIL → REJECT verdict bypasses expensive verification step
4. If 1-2 claims lack citations, run the live-verify anyway (lower signal-to-noise)
5. If all claims cite reproducible artifacts, proceed to full primary-source check

**Why auto-FAIL not just downgrade**: across multiple prior live A/B verifications of fabrication-pattern candidates, none survived verification. The prior probability of "≥3 unsourced claims yet verifiable" is empirically near-zero. Running the bench is budget-wasting ritual.

**Three-tier evidence-density ladder** (illustrative exemplars; "evidence-density gradient" = how much reproducible methodology sits behind the numeric claim):

- **Tier 0** (auto-FAIL): `tirth8205/code-review-graph` — ≥3 README numeric claims lack a live methodology artifact; claimed `evaluate/reports/summary.md` absent in verified checkout (numeric claims live in diagrams/tables/narrative) [VERIFIED 2026-05-03 via `Z:/repos/deps/code-review-graph/README.md:32,68,91-104,120-123,146-159,183-194 @ HEAD 0919071a9ba353e604981059e99ee2ed98768092`].
- **Tier 2** (strong-PASS): `context-mode` — `BENCHMARK.md` with 21 fixtures + repro instructions [VERIFIED 2026-05-03 via `Z:/repos/deps/context-mode/BENCHMARK.md:1-16,36-49,59-72,140-172 @ HEAD e40102e534632b137d2b9425b5ddd318222d114e`].
- **Tier 1** (mega-exemplar): `garrytan/gbrain` + `garrytan/gbrain-evals` — separate `gbrain-evals` repo with versioned benchmark scorecards [VERIFIED 2026-05-03 against `gbrain-evals` commit `b8cf8ad057635cbb03c0f3996acb693afbcae605`; see `README.md:5-10,25-45,88-120,151+`, `docs/benchmarks/2026-04-19-brainbench-v0_11-vs-v0_12.md:3-8,35-36,85-99`, `docs/benchmarks/2026-04-18-brainbench-v1.md:6-20,82-106,240-273`].

Use the tier only as a routing hint:
- **Tier 1** (dedicated eval repo): route to §procedure step 5; cite the eval repo and benchmark scorecard.
- **Tier 2** (inline benchmark with real fixtures): route to §procedure step 5; cite the benchmark file and repro commands.
- **Tier 0** (≥3 unsupported numeric claims or broken methodology links): §procedure step 3 AUTO-FAILS; do not spend live-verify budget.

## Even-shorter-path: upstream self-flags impostor-domain risk

If a README opens with a `[!CAUTION] Scam alert` block warning of impostor domains distributing malware, that's a STRONG axis-2 self-flag — the upstream itself is signaling the candidate exists in a high-volume scam-attractor space (typically a viral-young repo where bad actors create lookalike forks). Auto-REJECT axis-2 even before counting unsourced numeric claims.

**How to apply**: when scanning a README for axis-2 named-practitioner endorsements, also scan the FIRST 10 lines for `Scam alert` / `[!CAUTION]` blocks warning of impostor domains. If present, the candidate has been or currently is in a high-attention attractor space — REJECT-until-convergence even if other axes look strong (the self-flag indicates ongoing trust-volatility).

**Distinguishes from §Anti-pattern Row-2 fabrication-test FAIL**: that rule fires on candidate AUTHORS publishing unsourced claims (deception via omission). This rule fires on candidate AUTHORS warning about THIRD-PARTY impostors (deception attractor signal). Same auto-REJECT axis-2 verdict, different mechanism.

## Sibling-rule notes

- `.claude/rules/research-protocol.md` §Benchmark-first sub-rule cites this rule's Axis-3 5-band table as the convergence-gate for benchmark-derived adoption candidates. This port satisfies that forward-ref.
- `.claude/rules/evidence-policy.md` Marker Decay corollary: **Marker Decay is claim freshness at reuse time** ("[VERIFIED yesterday] may be [INFERRED] today if upstream changed"). **Convergence-gate Axis 3 stability is adoption maturity** ("repo / pattern is old enough to trust as ADOPT-NOW"). The two are conceptually adjacent — both touch on temporal aspects of evidence — but they are NOT substitutes:
  - Marker Decay: "is THIS marked-claim still valid?"
  - Axis 3 stability: "is THIS candidate mature enough to adopt?"
  - A claim can be [VERIFIED today] (Marker Decay PASS) about a young repo (Axis 3 FAIL).
- `.claude/rules/synthesis-layer-verify.md` §Subclaim-type discriminator is a different axis-shape (claim-source classification, see §Terminology disambiguation above).
